*** 9.13 Why doesn't my Meta key work in an =xterm= window? [[#Why-doesn_0027t-my-Meta-key-work-in-an-xterm-window_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: Why-doesn_0027t-my-Meta-key-work-in-an-xterm-window_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
See
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/emacs.html#Unibyte-Mode][Single-Byte Character Set Support]]
in The GNU Emacs Manual.

If the advice in the Emacs manual fails, try all of these methods before
asking for further help:

- You may have big problems using =mwm= as your window manager. (Does
  anyone know a good generic solution to allow the use of the Meta key
  in Emacs with =mwm=?)

- For X11: Make sure it really is a Meta key. Use =xev= to find out what
  keysym your Meta key generates. It should be either =Meta_L= or
  =Meta_R=. If it isn't, use =xmodmap= to fix the situation. If Meta
  does generate =Meta_L= or =Meta_R=, but M-x produces a non-ASCII
  character, put this in your =~/.Xdefaults= file:

  #+begin_src example-preformatted
   XTerm*eightBitInput:   false
   XTerm*eightBitOutput:  true
  #+end_src

- Make sure the =pty= the =xterm= is using is passing 8 bit characters.
  ‘=stty -a=' (or ‘=stty everything=') should show ‘=cs8=' somewhere. If
  it shows ‘=cs7=' instead, use ‘=stty cs8 -istrip=' (or ‘=stty pass8=')
  to fix it.

- If there is an =rlogin= connection between =xterm= and Emacs, the
  ‘=-8=' argument may need to be given to rlogin to make it pass all 8
  bits of every character.

- If all else fails, you can make =xterm= generate ESC W when you type
  M-W, which is the same conversion Emacs would make if it got the M-W
  anyway. In X11R4, the following resource specification will do this:

  #+begin_src example-preformatted
  XTerm.VT100.EightBitInput: false
  #+end_src

  (This changes the behavior of the =insert-eight-bit= action.)

  With older =xterm=s, you can specify this behavior with a translation:

  #+begin_src example-preformatted
  XTerm.VT100.Translations: #override \
    Meta<KeyPress>: string(0x1b) insert()
  #+end_src

  You might have to replace ‘=Meta=' with ‘=Alt='.

--------------

<<Some-Ctrl_002dmodified-keys-do-not-work-on-xterm>>

Previous:
[[#Meta-key-does-not-work-in-xterm][Why doesn't my Meta key work in an =xterm= window?]],
Up: [[#Key-bindings][Key bindings]]  
[[[#SEC_Contents][Contents]]][[[#Concept-index][Index]]]

*** 9.14 Why don't some keys like C-. work on xterm? [[#Why-don_0027t-some-keys-like-C_002d_002e-work-on-xterm_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: Why-don_0027t-some-keys-like-C_002d_002e-work-on-xterm_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
If your =xterm= version is 216 or newer, you should have keys like C-.
and C-, if you add the following resource specification to your
=~/.Xdefaults=:

#+begin_src example-preformatted
 XTerm.VT100.modifyOtherKeys: 1
#+end_src

If you want to use =uxterm=, also add the following:

#+begin_src example-preformatted
 UXTerm.VT100.modifyOtherKeys: 1
#+end_src

--------------

<<Alternate-character-sets>>

*** 10.1 How do I make Emacs display 8-bit characters? [[#How-do-I-make-Emacs-display-8_002dbit-characters_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-make-Emacs-display-8_002dbit-characters_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
See
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/emacs.html#Unibyte-Mode][Single-byte Character Set Support]]
in The GNU Emacs Manual. On a Unix, when Emacs runs on a text-only
terminal display or is invoked with ‘=emacs -nw=', you typically need to
use =set-terminal-coding-system= to tell Emacs what the terminal can
display, even after setting the language environment; otherwise
non-ASCII characters will display as ‘=?='. On other operating systems,
such as MS-Windows and MS-DOS, Emacs queries the OS about the character
set supported by the display, and sets up the required terminal coding
system automatically.

--------------

<<Inputting-eight_002dbit-characters>>

[[#Right_002dto_002dleft-alphabets][Where is an Emacs that can handle Semitic (right-to-left) alphabets?]],

*** 10.2 How do I input eight-bit characters? [[#How-do-I-input-eight_002dbit-characters_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-input-eight_002dbit-characters_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
Various methods are available for input of eight-bit characters. See
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/emacs.html#Unibyte-Mode][Single-byte Character Set Support]]
in The GNU Emacs Manual. For more sophisticated methods, see
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/emacs.html#Input-Methods][Input Methods]]
in The GNU Emacs Manual.

--------------

*** 10.3 Where is an Emacs that can handle Semitic (right-to-left) alphabets? [[#Where-is-an-Emacs-that-can-handle-Semitic-_0028right_002dto_002dleft_0029-alphabets_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: Where-is-an-Emacs-that-can-handle-Semitic-_0028right_002dto_002dleft_0029-alphabets_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
Emacs supports display and editing of bidirectional scripts, such as
Arabic, Farsi, and Hebrew.

--------------

*** 10.4 How do I add fonts for use with Emacs? [[#How-do-I-add-fonts-for-use-with-Emacs_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-add-fonts-for-use-with-Emacs_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
First, download and install the BDF font files and any auxiliary
packages they need. The GNU Intlfonts distribution can be found on
[[https://directory.fsf.org/localization/intlfonts.html][the GNU Software Directory Web site]].

Next, if you are on X Window system, issue the following two commands
from the shell's prompt:

#+begin_src example-preformatted
  xset +fp /usr/local/share/emacs/fonts
  xset fp rehash
#+end_src

(Modify the first command if you installed the fonts in a directory that
is not =/usr/local/share/emacs/fonts=.) You also need to arrange for
these two commands to run whenever you log in, e.g., by adding them to
your window-system startup file, such as =~/.xsessionrc= or
=~/.gnomerc=.

Now, add the following line to your init file (see
[[#Setting-up-a-customization-file][How do I set up an init file properly?]]):

#+begin_src lisp-preformatted
  (add-to-list 'bdf-directory-list "/usr/share/emacs/fonts/bdf")
#+end_src

(Again, modify the file name if you installed the fonts elsewhere.)

Finally, if you wish to use the installed fonts with =ps-print=, add the
following line to your init file:

#+begin_src lisp-preformatted
  (setq ps-multibyte-buffer 'bdf-font-except-latin)
#+end_src

You can now use the Emacs font menu to select the ‘=bdf: 16-dot medium='
fontset, or you can select it by setting the default font in your init
file:

#+begin_src lisp-preformatted
  (set-frame-font "fontset-bdf")
#+end_src

*** 11.1 How do I change the included text prefix in mail/news followups? [[#How-do-I-change-the-included-text-prefix-in-mail_002fnews-followups_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-change-the-included-text-prefix-in-mail_002fnews-followups_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
If you read mail with Rmail, set the variable =mail-yank-prefix=. For
Gnus, set =message-yank-prefix=. For VM, set =vm-included-text-prefix=.
For mh-e, set =mh-ins-buf-prefix=.

For fancier control of citations, use Supercite (see
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/sc.html#Top][the Supercite Manual]]
in The Supercite Manual).

To prevent Emacs from including various headers of the replied-to
message, set the value of =mail-yank-ignored-headers= to an appropriate
regexp.

--------------

<<Saving-a-copy-of-outgoing-mail>>

[[#Expanding-aliases-when-sending-mail][Why doesn't Emacs expand my aliases when sending mail?]],

Up: [[#Mail-and-news][Mail and news]]  
[[[#SEC_Contents][Contents]]][[[#Concept-index][Index]]]

*** 11.2 How do I save a copy of outgoing mail? [[#How-do-I-save-a-copy-of-outgoing-mail_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-save-a-copy-of-outgoing-mail_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
You can either mail yourself a copy by including a ‘=BCC=' header in the
mail message, or store a copy of the message directly to a file by
including an ‘=FCC=' header.

If you use standard mail, you can automatically create a ‘=BCC=' to
yourself by putting

#+begin_src lisp-preformatted
(setq mail-self-blind t)
#+end_src

in your init file (see
[[#Setting-up-a-customization-file][How do I set up an init file properly?]]).
You can automatically include an ‘=FCC=' field by putting something like
the following in your init file:

#+begin_src lisp-preformatted
(setq mail-archive-file-name (expand-file-name "~/outgoing"))
#+end_src

The output file will be in Unix mail format.

If you use =mh-e=, add an ‘=FCC=' or ‘=BCC=' field to your components
file.

It does not work to put ‘=set record filename=' in the =.mailrc= file.

--------------

<<Expanding-aliases-when-sending-mail>>

*** 11.3 Why doesn't Emacs expand my aliases when sending mail? [[#Why-doesn_0027t-Emacs-expand-my-aliases-when-sending-mail_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: Why-doesn_0027t-Emacs-expand-my-aliases-when-sending-mail_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
See
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/emacs.html#Mail-Aliases][The Emacs Manual]]
in The Emacs Manual.

- Normally, Emacs expands aliases when you send the message. To expand
  them before this, use M-x expand-mail-aliases.

- Emacs normally only reads the =.mailrc= file once per session, when
  you start to compose your first mail message. If you edit the file
  after this, you can use M-x build-mail-aliases to make Emacs reread
  it.

- If you like, you can expand mail aliases as abbrevs, as soon as you
  type them in. To enable this feature, execute the following:

  #+begin_src lisp-preformatted
  (add-hook 'mail-mode-hook 'mail-abbrevs-setup)
  #+end_src

  Note that the aliases are expanded automatically only after you type a
  word-separator character (e.g., RET or ,). You can force their
  expansion by moving point to the end of the alias and typing C-x a e
  (M-x expand-abbrev).

--------------

*** 11.5 Why does Rmail need to write to =/var/spool/mail=? [[#Why-does-Rmail-need-to-write-to-_002fvar_002fspool_002fmail_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: Why-does-Rmail-need-to-write-to-_002fvar_002fspool_002fmail_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
This is the behavior of the =movemail= program which Rmail uses. This
indicates that =movemail= is configured to use lock files.

RMS writes:

#+begin_quote
Certain systems require lock files to interlock access to mail files. On
these systems, =movemail= must write lock files, or you risk losing
mail. You simply must arrange to let =movemail= write them.

Other systems use the =flock= system call to interlock access. On these
systems, you should configure =movemail= to use =flock=.
#+end_quote

--------------

<<Replying-to-the-sender-of-a-message>>

*** 11.6 How can I force Rmail to reply to the sender of a message, but not the other recipients? [[#How-can-I-force-Rmail-to-reply-to-the-sender-of-a-message_002c-but-not-the-other-recipients_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-can-I-force-Rmail-to-reply-to-the-sender-of-a-message_002c-but-not-the-other-recipients_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
Ron Isaacson says: When you hit r to reply in Rmail, by default it Ccs
all of the original recipients (everyone on the original ‘=To=' and
‘=CC=' lists). With a prefix argument (i.e., typing C-u before r), it
replies only to the sender. However, going through the whole C-u
business every time you want to reply is a pain. This is the best fix
I've been able to come up with:

#+begin_src lisp-preformatted
(defun rmail-reply-t ()
  "Reply only to the sender of the current message.  (See rmail-reply.)"
  (interactive)
  (rmail-reply t))

(add-hook 'rmail-mode-hook
  (lambda ()
    (keymap-set rmail-mode-map "r" 'rmail-reply-t)
    (keymap-set rmail-mode-map "R" 'rmail-reply)))
#+end_src

--------------

<<Automatically-starting-a-mail-or-news-reader>>

*** 11.7 How do I make Emacs automatically start my mail/news reader? [[#How-do-I-make-Emacs-automatically-start-my-mail_002fnews-reader_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-make-Emacs-automatically-start-my-mail_002fnews-reader_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
To start Emacs in Gnus:

#+begin_src example-preformatted
emacs -f gnus
#+end_src

in Rmail:

#+begin_src example-preformatted
emacs -f rmail
#+end_src

A more convenient way to start with Gnus:

#+begin_src example-preformatted
alias gnus 'emacs -f gnus'
gnus
#+end_src

It is probably unwise to automatically start your mail or news reader
from your init file. This would cause problems if you needed to run two
copies of Emacs at the same time. Also, this would make it difficult for
you to start Emacs quickly when you needed to.

--------------

*** 11.8 How do I read news under Emacs? [[#How-do-I-read-news-under-Emacs_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-read-news-under-Emacs_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
Use M-x gnus. For more information on Gnus, see
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/gnus.html#Top][the Gnus Manual]]
in The Gnus Manual, which includes
[[https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_mono/gnus.html#Frequently-Asked-Questions][the Gnus FAQ]]
in The Gnus Manual.

*** 11.9 How do I make Gnus faster? [[#How-do-I-make-Gnus-faster_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-make-Gnus-faster_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
From the Gnus FAQ (see
[[#Reading-news-with-Emacs][How do I read news under Emacs?]]):

#+begin_quote
If you have a slow machine, or are just really impatient, there are a
few things you can do to make Gnus run faster.

Set =gnus-check-new-newsgroups= and =gnus-check-bogus-newsgroups= to
=nil= to make startup faster.

Set =gnus-show-threads=, =gnus-use-cross-reference= and
=gnus-nov-is-evil= to =nil= to make entering and exiting the summary
buffer faster.
#+end_quote

--------------

<<Catching-up-in-all-newsgroups>>

[[#Mail-and-news][Mail and news]]

*** 11.10 How do I catch up all newsgroups in Gnus? [[#How-do-I-catch-up-all-newsgroups-in-Gnus_003f][¶]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: How-do-I-catch-up-all-newsgroups-in-Gnus_003f
:CLASS: section
:END:
In the =*Newsgroup*= buffer, type M-< C-x ( c y C-x ) M-0 C-x e

Leave off the initial M-< if you only want to catch up from point to the
end of the =*Newsgroup*= buffer.

**** Footnotes

***** [[#DOCF1][(1)]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: section
:CLASS: footnote-body-heading
:END:
DOS and Windows terminals don't set bit 7 when the Meta key is pressed.

***** [[#DOCF2][(2)]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: section-1
:CLASS: footnote-body-heading
:END:
For more information, see
[[https://www.gnu.org/licenses/why-assign.html][Why the FSF Gets Copyright Assignments from Contributors]].

***** [[#DOCF3][(3)]]
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: section-2
:CLASS: footnote-body-heading
:END:
It used to be an official place where people could post or announce
their extensions to Emacs. That is still allowed, but exceedingly rare
these days.
